<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_groups">Ethnic groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Togo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Ethnic groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Togo-benin_1985-079_hg.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Togo-benin_1985-079_hg.jpg/220px-Togo-benin_1985-079_hg.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Togo-benin_1985-079_hg.jpg/330px-Togo-benin_1985-079_hg.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Togo-benin_1985-079_hg.jpg/440px-Togo-benin_1985-079_hg.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3943" data-file-height="2645" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Togo-benin_1985-079_hg.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>People in the 1980s</div></div></div>
<p>In Togo, there are about 40 different ethnic groups, the most numerous of which are the <a href="/wiki/Ewe_people" title="Ewe people">Ewe</a> in the south who make up 32% of the population. Along the southern coastline, they account for 21% of the population. Also found are <a href="/wiki/Tem_language" title="Tem language">Kotokoli or Tem</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tchamba" title="Tchamba">Tchamba</a> in the center and the <a href="/wiki/Kabye_people" title="Kabye people">Kabye people</a> in the north (22%). The <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ouatchi&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ouatchi (page does not exist)">Ouatchis</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waci_(peuple)" class="extiw" title="fr:Waci (peuple)">fr</a>&#93;</span> are 14% of the population. Sometimes the Ewes and Ouatchis are considered the same, while the French who studied both groups considered them different people.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
Other ethnic groups include the Mina, <a href="/wiki/Mossi_people" title="Mossi people">Mossi</a>, the Moba and Bassar, the Tchokossi of Mango (about 8%).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Togo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Togo" title="Religion in Togo">Religion in Togo</a></div>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">
<div class="mw-no-invert" style="background-color:white;margin:auto;position:relative;width:200px;height:200px;overflow:hidden;border-radius:100px;border:1px solid black;transform:scaleX(-1)rotate(-90deg)">
<div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:0; top:0; border-width:0 200px 200px 0; border-color:Black"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 2651.3256287233px; border-left-color:Grey"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Grey"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Grey"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 219.63310880999px; border-left-color:Green"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Green"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Green"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 27.019522207315px; border-left-color:Beige"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Beige"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Beige"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;right:100px; top:0; border-width:0 190.31049331139px 100px 0; border-right-color:Blue"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;right:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 100px 0;border-color:Blue"></div>
</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>Religion in Togo
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Blue; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> (42.3%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Beige; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Traditional_African_religions" title="Traditional African religions">Traditional faiths</a> (36.9%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Green; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> (14%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Grey; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Irreligion" title="Irreligion">No religion</a> (6.2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Black; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other (0.6%)</div>
</div>
</div></div><p><sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup></p><div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kpalime.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Kpalime.jpg/220px-Kpalime.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Kpalime.jpg/330px-Kpalime.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Kpalime.jpg/440px-Kpalime.jpg 2x" data-file-width="460" data-file-height="303" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kpalime.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Church in <a href="/wiki/Kpalime" class="mw-redirect" title="Kpalime">Kpalime</a></div></div></div>
<p>According to a 2012 US government religious freedoms report, in 2004 the University of Lomé estimated that 33% of the population were traditional animists, 28% were Roman Catholic, 20% Sunni Muslim, 9% Protestant and another 5% belonged to other Christian denominations. The remaining 5% were reported to include persons not affiliated with any religious group. The report noted that "many" Christians and Muslims continue to perform indigenous religious practices.<sup id="cite_ref-Togo_2012_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Togo_2012-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> The CIA World Factbook meanwhile states that 44% of the population are Christian, 14% are Muslim with 36% being followers of indigenous beliefs.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_58-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> began to spread from the middle of the 15th century, after the arrival of Portuguese <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Catholic</a> missionaries. Germans introduced <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestantism</a> in the second half of the 19th century when a hundred missionaries of the Bremen Missionary Society were sent to the coastal areas of Togo and Ghana. Togo's Protestants were known as "Brema", a corruption of the word "<a href="/wiki/Bremen" title="Bremen">Bremen</a>". After <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, German missionaries had to leave, which gave birth to the early autonomy of the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ewe_Evangelical_Church&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ewe Evangelical Church (page does not exist)">Ewe Evangelical Church</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Togo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Togo" title="Languages of Togo">Languages of Togo</a></div>
<p>According to Ethnologue, 39 distinct languages are spoken in the country, some of them by communities that number fewer than 100,000 members.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> Of the 39 languages, the sole <a href="/wiki/Official_language" title="Official language">official language</a> is French.<sup id="cite_ref-iccia.com_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-iccia.com-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Two spoken indigenous languages were designated politically as <a href="/wiki/National_language" title="National language">national languages</a> in 1975: <a href="/wiki/Ewe_language" title="Ewe language">Ewé</a> (<a href="/wiki/Ewe_language" title="Ewe language">Ewe</a>: <i lang="ee">Èʋegbe</i>; <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">Evé</i>) and <a href="/wiki/Kabiy%C3%A9_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Kabiyé language">Kabiyé</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-iccia.com_71-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-iccia.com-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>French is used in formal education, legislature, all forms of media, <a href="/wiki/Administration_(law)" title="Administration (law)">administration</a> and commerce. Ewe is a language of wider communication in the south. <a href="/wiki/Tem_language" title="Tem language">Tem</a> functions to a limited extent as a trade language in some northern towns.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Officially, Ewe and Kabiye are "national languages", which in the Togolese context means languages that are promoted in formal education and used in the media. In joining the Commonwealth, the Togolese government has anticipated opportunities for Togolese citizens to learn English.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_25-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Togo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Human Rights Measurement Initiative<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> finds that Togo is fulfilling 73.1% of what it should be fulfilling for the right to health based on its level of income.<sup id="cite_ref-Togo_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Togo_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> When looking at the right to health with respect to children, Togo achieves 93.8% of what is expected based on its current income.<sup id="cite_ref-Togo_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker_74-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Togo_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> In regards to the right to health amongst the adult population, the country achieves 88.2% of what is expected based on the nation's level of income.<sup id="cite_ref-Togo_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker_74-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Togo_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> It falls into the "very bad" category when evaluating the right to reproductive health because the nation is fulfilling 37.3% of what the nation is expected to achieve based on the resources (income) it has available.<sup id="cite_ref-Togo_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker_74-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Togo_-_HRMI_Rights_Tracker-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Health expenditure in Togo was 5.2% of GDP in 2014, which ranks the country in 45th place in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_58-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Infant_mortality" title="Infant mortality">infant mortality</a> rate is approximately 43.7 deaths per 1,000 children in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_58-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Male <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy_at_birth" class="mw-redirect" title="Life expectancy at birth">life expectancy at birth</a> was at 62.3 in 2016, whereas it was at 67.7 years for females.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_58-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> There were 5 physicians per 100,000 people in 2008<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_58-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> According to a 2013 UNICEF report,<sup id="cite_ref-UNICEF2013p27_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNICEF2013p27-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> 4% of women in Togo have undergone <a href="/wiki/Female_genital_mutilation" title="Female genital mutilation">female genital mutilation</a>.
</p><p>As of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Togo&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the maternal mortality rate per 100,000 births for Togo is 368, compared with 350 in 2010 and 539.7 in 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_58-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> The under 5 mortality rate per 1,000 births is 100, and the neonatal mortality as a percentage of under 5's mortality is 32. In Togo the number of midwives per 1,000 live births is 2 and the lifetime risk of death for pregnant women is 1 in 67.<sup id="cite_ref-SOWMY_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SOWMY-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2016, Togo had 4100 (2400–6100) new HIV infections and 5100 (3100–7700) AIDS-related deaths. There were 100,000 (73,000–130,000) people living with HIV in 2016, among whom 51% (37–67%) were accessing antiretroviral therapy. Among pregnant women living with HIV, 86% (59% – &gt;95%) were accessing treatment or prophylaxis to prevent transmission of HIV to their children. An estimated &lt;1000 (&lt;500–1400) children were newly infected with HIV due to mother-to-child transmission. Among people living with HIV, approximately 42% (30–55%) had suppressed viral loads.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Togo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Education_in_Togo" title="Education in Togo">Education in Togo</a> is compulsory for six years.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> In 1996, the gross primary enrollment rate was 119.6%, and the net primary enrollment rate was 81.3%.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, the net enrollment rate was 94%. The education system has "suffered from teacher shortages, lower educational quality in rural areas, and high repetition and dropout rates".<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_78-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Togo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Togo" title="Culture of Togo">Culture of Togo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Togo" title="Music of Togo">Music of Togo</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Togo_Taberma_house_04.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Togo_Taberma_house_04.jpg/220px-Togo_Taberma_house_04.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Togo_Taberma_house_04.jpg/330px-Togo_Taberma_house_04.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Togo_Taberma_house_04.jpg/440px-Togo_Taberma_house_04.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="1944" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Togo_Taberma_house_04.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Taberma houses</div></div></div>
<p>The culture reflects the influences of ethnic groups, the largest of which are the <a href="/wiki/Ewe_people" title="Ewe people">Ewe</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mina_people&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Mina people (page does not exist)">Mina</a>, Tem, Tchamba and <a href="/wiki/Kabre" class="mw-redirect" title="Kabre">Kabre</a>. Some people follow native <a href="/wiki/Animistic" class="mw-redirect" title="Animistic">animistic</a> practices and beliefs.
</p><p>Ewe statuary is characterized by its <a href="/wiki/Statuettes" class="mw-redirect" title="Statuettes">statuettes</a> which illustrate the worship of the <a href="/wiki/Ibeji" title="Ibeji">ibeji</a>. Sculptures and hunting trophies were used rather than the "more ubiquitous" African masks. The wood-carvers of <a href="/wiki/Kloto" class="mw-redirect" title="Kloto">Kloto</a> has their "chains of marriage": Two characters are connected by rings whittled from one piece of wood.
</p><p>The dyed fabric <a href="/wiki/Batik" title="Batik">batiks</a> of the artisanal center of Kloto represent stylized and colored scenes of ancient everyday life. There are loincloths used in the ceremonies of the weavers of Assahoun. Works of the painter Sokey Edorh are inspired by the "immense arid extents, swept by the dry wind", and where the soil keeps the prints of the men and the animals. The plastics technician <a href="/wiki/Paul_Ahyi" title="Paul Ahyi">Paul Ahyi</a> practiced the "zota", a kind of pyroengraving, and his monumental achievements decorate <a href="/wiki/Lom%C3%A9" title="Lomé">Lomé</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">Basketball</a> is Togo's "second most practiced sport".<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Togo featured a national team in <a href="/wiki/Beach_volleyball" title="Beach volleyball">beach volleyball</a> that competed at the <a href="/wiki/2018%E2%80%932020_CAVB_Beach_Volleyball_Continental_Cup" title="2018–2020 CAVB Beach Volleyball Continental Cup">2018–2020 CAVB Beach Volleyball Continental Cup</a> in the men's section.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mass media includes radio, television, and online and print formats. The <a href="/w/index.php?title=Agence_Togolaise_de_Presse&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Agence Togolaise de Presse (page does not exist)">Agence Togolaise de Presse</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agence_togolaise_de_presse" class="extiw" title="fr:Agence togolaise de presse">fr</a>&#93;</span> news agency began in 1975.<sup id="cite_ref-Europ2003_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Europ2003-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> The Union des Journalistes Independants du Togo press association is headquartered in Lomé.<sup id="cite_ref-Europ2003_81-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Europ2003-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Togo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1132942124">.mw-parser-output .portalbox{padding:0;margin:0.5em 0;display:table;box-sizing:border-box;max-width:175px;list-style:none}.mw-parser-output .portalborder{border:solid #aaa 1px;padding:0.1em;background:#f9f9f9}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-entry{display:table-row;font-size:85%;line-height:110%;height:1.9em;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-image{display:table-cell;padding:0.2em;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-link{display:table-cell;padding:0.2em 0.2em 0.2em 0.3em;vertical-align:middle}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .portalleft{clear:left;float:left;margin:0.5em 1em 0.5em 0}.mw-parser-output .portalright{clear:right;float:right;margin:0.5em 0 0.5em 1em}}</style><ul role="navigation" aria-label="Portals" class="noprint portalbox portalborder portalright">
<li class="portalbox-entry"><span class="portalbox-image"><img alt="map" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Africa_%28orthographic_projection%29.svg/28px-Africa_%28orthographic_projection%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="28" height="28" class="noviewer" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Africa_%28orthographic_projection%29.svg/42px-Africa_%28orthographic_projection%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Africa_%28orthographic_projection%29.svg/56px-Africa_%28orthographic_projection%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="550" data-file-height="550" /></span><span class="portalbox-link"><a href="/wiki/Portal:Africa" title="Portal:Africa">Africa portal</a></span></li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Togo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
